# References

> **Citations written `pre-clean-slate:<path>`** name files deleted in the
> 2026-09-28 clean slate. Read one with `git show pre-clean-slate:<path>`;
> `docs/HISTORY.md` explains. They are provenance, not current state.

Every external source the 2026-09-09 research pass established, with the claim
it supports and its verification status. **Prompts and notes cite this file
rather than re-deriving.** When a prompt's result changes what a source is used
for, amend the row here rather than leaving two versions in circulation.

Status key:
- **[V]** verified this session against a primary source that was actually fetched
- **[S]** sourced — a real, identified publication or page, but not fetched and
  re-read this session
- **[U]** uncertain — the claim is directionally supported but the number, the
  citation details, or both could not be confirmed. **Do not put a [U] number
  into a model without pulling the primary source first.**

---

## 1. Closed-end fund discounts: the foundational literature

| claim | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| Discounts co-move across funds; CEF IPOs cluster when seasoned funds trade near par; discounts correlate with other sentiment-sensitive assets | Lee, Shleifer & Thaler (1991), *J. Finance* 46:75–109 | [S] |
| Dividend yield and replication risk confirmed as two of the arbitrage-cost proxies with the expected signs (via Gemmill & Thomas 2002, read in full). **The full four-proxy set, the coefficients, and the "~25% of cross-sectional variance" R² could NOT be verified** — JSTOR, Oxford Academic and every PDF mirror blocked | Pontiff (1996), *QJE* 111(4):1135–1151 | **[U]** on magnitudes |
| Funds at premiums accrue negative abnormal returns, funds at discounts positive — direction confirmed by two papers citing it. **The "20% discount → ~6pp over 12 months" magnitude could NOT be verified** (ScienceDirect 403, no OA copy, no citing paper quotes the coefficient). Treat the number as unconfirmed | Pontiff (1995), *JFE* 37(3):341–370 | **[U]** on magnitude |
| **~85% of CEF excess volatility is idiosyncratic** to a four-factor model; idiosyncratic risk, not transaction cost, is the binding arbitrage constraint | Pontiff (2006), *J. Accounting & Economics* | [S] |
| CEF value = NAV + capitalised liquidity benefit − capitalised fees; explains why illiquid-asset CEFs can trade at *smaller* discounts | Cherkes, Sagi & Stanton (2009), *RFS* 22(1):257–297 | [S] |
| Discount = PV(fees) − PV(manager value added), with a firing threshold generating the IPO-to-discount lifecycle | Berk & Stanton (2007), *J. Finance* 62(2):529–556 | [S] |
| Management fees as a derivative claim on NAV; a non-behavioural rationalisation of the discount | Ross (2002), *European Financial Management* | [S] |
| Noise-trader sentiment drives short-run discount moves but is **rejected** as the explanation of the long-run level (158 UK CEFs) | Gemmill & Thomas (2002), *J. Finance* 57:2571–2594 | [S] |
| Payout level and stability signal sustainable NAV growth; higher, steadier payout → smaller discount | Johnson, Lin & Song (2006), *JFE* | [S] |
| Investor-sentiment proxies explain only **~7% of the variance** of weekly discount changes | Brauer (1993), *J. Financial Services Research* 7(3):199–216 | [S] |
| Abstract, verified verbatim: *"equity-based funds converge to the steady-state level faster than fixed income funds."* Method is "bias-corrected bootstrap half-life estimates" on UK and US CEFs. **Body, tables and actual half-life numbers are paywalled with no open-access copy anywhere** (Unpaywall: `is_oa: false`, no repository copy) | Ji & Kim (2013), *Applied Economics* 45(32):4503–4515 | [V] abstract only |
| **CONTRADICTS the above.** 377 US CEFs, Aug 1984–Dec 2011, monthly, per-fund ADF bias-corrected by 10,000-replication simulation: *"funds investing in fixed-income securities have faster speeds of reversion than funds investing in equities"* — Table III mean β −0.121 equity vs −0.136 fixed income | Patro, Piccotti & Wu (2014), "Exploiting Closed-End Fund Discounts" | [V] read in full |
| **Full-sample bias-adjusted half-life 7.7 months**; reversion coefficient ranges **−0.002 to −0.541 across 334 funds**; optimised quintile half-lives 4.1–4.9 months | Patro, Piccotti & Wu (2014) | [V] read in full |
| **The closest thing to a pooled-vs-per-fund horse race, and it favours per-fund**: strategies exploiting reversion-speed heterogeneity earn **Sharpe 1.86–1.92 against 1.52** for a naive premium-sign strategy — *"substantially improves trading strategy returns"*. **Monthly, and gross of costs** | Patro, Piccotti & Wu (2014) | [V] read in full |
| **The asset-class ordering is NOT settled** — two papers disagree, with different samples and methods. No prompt may assert a prior about it | — | [V] negative |
| Average CEF discount ~8.42% with ~98% period-to-period autocorrelation | Xu et al., "The Persistence and Predictability of Closed-End Fund Discounts" (UT Dallas) | [S] |

**The horizon gap, and it is the most important row in this table.** None of the
above operates at a 1–5 day horizon, and this was checked rather than assumed on
2026-09-09: a targeted search for daily or weekly CEF-discount half-life
estimates found **nothing published or in working-paper form**. Every locatable
study is monthly at best, with half-lives of 4–8 months. Our pooled AR(1)
φ = 0.9722 implies **24.6 days**. The negative result is itself the finding — any
two-day estimate has no precedent and must be derived in-house. That mismatch is
why `W4_artifact_battery.md` runs before any further trial.

## 2. Stale pricing, smoothing, and NAV mechanics

| claim | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| Observed returns are a moving average of true returns under stale marks; smoothing understates volatility and inflates Sharpe. The MA(q) unsmoothing framework | Getmansky, Lo & Makarov (2004), *JFE* 74(3):529–609 | [S] |
| Bond-fund NAVs are "extremely stale"; fund returns predictable over days to weeks, worse in crises; ~$1.2bn/yr dilution to buy-and-hold investors | Choi, Kronlund & Oh (2022), *JFE* 145(2):296–317, "Sitting Bucks" | [S] |
| Cross-fund dispersion in month-end marks on **identical** corporate bonds, consistent with return smoothing by managers — i.e. part of the smoothing is discretionary, not mechanical | Cici, Gibson & Merrick, "Missing the Marks", *JFE* | [S] |
| Muni market averages **under 2,800 trades and under 1,500 unique securities per day**; ~two-thirds of securities that trade at all trade once or twice; >1m CUSIPs outstanding | MSRB, "Trading Patterns in the Municipal Securities Market" (2024, 2023 data) | [V] |
| Muni NAVs are evaluated/matrix priced from comparable-bond curves, not last trades — sponsor's own language: *"yields or prices of municipal securities of comparable quality, type of issue, coupon, maturity and rating"* | Nuveen N-2/A (SEC EDGAR, CIK 883618) | [V] |
| ICE evaluates ~1.0m active US municipal bonds via a rules-based curve framework refreshed from MSRB RTRS round-lot reports | ICE Data Pricing & Reference Data, evaluated-pricing methodology | [V] |
| Bank-loan CEFs price from independent pricing services (dealer-quote surveys) approved by the board | Nuveen floating-rate N-CSR (EDGAR CIK 1276533) | [V] |
| **Rule 22c-1's forward-pricing mandate applies only to redeemable securities and therefore not to CEFs at all**; daily NAV striking is convention, not a clean statutory requirement | SEC, Rule 22c-1 amendments (2003) | [V] |
| Rule 2a-5 (adopted Dec 2020, **compliance 8 Sept 2022**) moved fair-valuation to a board-overseen "valuation designee" — a family's smoothing profile can change without announcement | SEC Release IC-34128 | [S] |
| CEFConnect **italicises a NAV whose published date is earlier than the row's date** — the aggregator itself flags carried-forward NAVs | cefconnect.com daily pricing page | [V] |
| On the ex-date **both NAV and price fall by the distribution**; the realised price drop is slightly less than the distribution due to tick discreteness | Bali & Hite (1998); mechanics per fund accounting | [S] |
| 2023 traditional-CEF distributions: **67% income, 13% capital gains, 20% return of capital** | ICI, "The Closed-End Fund Market, 2023" | [V] |
| Managed distribution policies require an SEC exemptive order under §19(b)/Rule 19b-1; **~32% of traditional CEFs** had one as of April 2024, concentrated outside credit | ICI, citing Morningstar | [V] |

|---|---|---|

## 6. Options, volatility, and hedging

| claim | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| Delta-hedged P&L identity `dΠ = ½ΓS²(r² − σ_imp²Δt)`; held to expiry it is a **gamma-weighted** average of realised variance, so path matters | standard; Carr & Madan (1998); Neuberger (1994) "The Log Contract", *JPM* | [S] |
| A variance swap is the pure vol instrument because the log contract has Γ = 1/S², making dollar gamma constant and variance weighting uniform | Carr & Madan (1998); Demeterfi et al. (1999, GS) | [S] |
| Discrete hedging error is proportional to Γ(ΔS² − σ²S²Δt), approximately **χ²₁**-distributed; **variance scales as 1/N, standard deviation as 1/√N**. Citation exact (DOI 10.1016/0304-405X(80)90003-3); **the primary text is paywalled and the numerical results could not be read** — the characterisation is how the paper is universally described, not a verified quote. G3 Part A verifies the 1/N scaling on our own path instead | Boyle & Emanuel (1980), *JFE* 8:259–282 | [V] citation / **[U]** internals |
| Whalley-Wilmott no-transaction band. **The distinctive result — H ∝ Γ^(2/3) — is safe and consistently cited. The full expression is NOT verified**, and an earlier version of our own spec had a **notation collision**, writing risk aversion as lowercase `γ` while capital `Γ` is the option gamma in the same formula (literature generally uses `λ`). Whether the spot term is `S` or `S²` is also unconfirmed. **Get the paper before coding the band** | Whalley & Wilmott (1997), *Mathematical Finance* | **[U]** |
| Leland's adjusted volatility `Le = √(2/π)·k/(σ√Δt)`; **Kabanov & Safarian (1997) showed the limiting hedging error is non-zero**, contradicting Leland's convergence claim | Leland (1985), *J. Finance* 40:1283–1301; Kabanov & Safarian (1997) | [S] |
| Zakamouline corrects WW's degenerate bandwidth away from the money; Hodges & Neuberger (1989) is the reference true optimum | Zakamouline (2006) | [S] |
| ATM asymptotics: `Γ ∝ 1/√T`, `Θ ∝ 1/√T`, `Vega ∝ √T`, `Γ/premium ∝ 1/T`, `|Θ|/Γ ≈ ½σ²S²` tenor-invariant; ATM premium ≈ 0.4Sσ√T | standard Black-Scholes; Brenner-Subrahmanyam | [S] |
| Daily breakeven move ≈ `S·σ_imp/√252` | derivation from the identity | [S] |
| **Arbitrage-free SVI**: butterfly (non-negative density) and calendar (total variance non-decreasing in T) conditions on the five raw SVI parameters; SSVI for surfaces | Gatheral & Jacquier (2013), *Quantitative Finance*; arXiv:1204.0646 | [V] |
| SPY implied ÷ forward realised variance: **median 1.32, mean 1.55** in this repo's own data | `data/vrp/vrp_monthly.csv` | [V] internal |
| **DO NOT CITE — the "−74% average synthetic variance-swap payoff across ETFs including HYG" could NOT be verified.** Authorship (Aragon, Chen & Shi) and AFA 2025 venue confirmed via the authors' own pages; SSRN is bot-blocked and no abstract is indexed by CrossRef, Semantic Scholar or Unpaywall. Use Carr & Wu below instead — verified, and it makes the same point | Aragon, Chen & Shi | **[U] — unusable** |
| **Average log variance risk premium −66%** on 30-day S&P 500 variance swaps; annualised Sharpe on *shorting* variance **0.98 (S&P 500), 0.85 (S&P 100), 0.87 (Dow)**. Sample Jan 1996 – Feb 2003, five indices and 35 single names | Carr & Wu (2009), *RFS* 22(3):1311–1341 | [V] read in full |
| **The premium is an index phenomenon far more than a single-name one:** only **7 of 35 single names** show a significantly negative premium in levels (23 of 35 in logs), against all three major indices | Carr & Wu (2009) | [V] |
| **Credit VRP, all three figures confirmed exactly against the primary PDF:** firm VRP alone explains **34%** of cross-sectional 5-year CDS spread variation (adjusted R²), **49%** with controls; mean VRP rises monotonically **from 7 at AAA to 82 at CCC** (monthly %²); estimated price of market VRP risk **λ = 1.17 against an observed 1.20**. Sample Jan 2001 – Sep 2008, 382 firms. **The cleanest citation in the options programme** | Wang, Zhou & Zhou (2011), Federal Reserve FEDS 2011-02 | [V] read in full |
| Verbatim from the abstract: *"This smirk is more pronounced in the credit market than in the equity market."* Higher-leverage and larger firms have lower VRP. **Magnitudes paywalled** | Kita & Tortorice (2021), *J. Corporate Finance* 69 | [V] abstract |
| VRP predicts aggregate excess returns, concentrated at the **quarterly** horizon. **CORRECTION: the headline univariate quarterly adjusted R² is 6.82%, not the "low-to-mid teens" recorded earlier** — teens are reachable only in multivariate specifications adding P/E, term spread and the relative short rate (16.76%, up to 21.81%). Sample S&P 500, Jan 1990 – Dec 2007 | Bollerslev, Tauchen & Zhou (2009), *RFS* 22(11):4463–4492 | [V] read in full |

| Splitting VIX² into expected-variance and premium components: the **premium** part predicts returns, the expected-variance part predicts activity | Bekaert & Hoerova (2014), *J. Econometrics* | [S] |
| **≈74% of the variance risk premium is compensation for jump/tail risk, not diffusive risk** — left-tail component 88.4% of the average premium against 14.8% right-tail; the authors' own words are *"close to three-quarters... may be attributed to investor fears."* Median equity jump/tail premium 5.2% against a total ERP near 8%, so **~two-thirds of the equity premium is tail compensation too.** This is the structural argument against timing | Bollerslev & Todorov (2011), *J. Finance* 66(6) | [V] read in full |

Sample Jan 1996 – Dec 2006. **The documented edge is in selling into an implied run-up**, which is why no event conditioner is pre-registered | Goyal & Saretto (2009), *JFE* 94(2) | [V] read in full |

## 7. Statistical method

| claim | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| Shrinkage dominates the per-entity MLE in total MSE for m ≥ 3 means | James & Stein (1961); Efron & Morris (1975), *JASA* | [V] |
| Empirical-Bayes posterior mean `θ̂_i = w_i·y_i + (1−w_i)·μ̂` with **`w_i = τ²/(τ² + se_i²)`** | Morris (1983), *JASA* 78; Gelman et al., *BDA3* Ch. 5 | [S] |
| **With few groups, use a weakly-informative prior on τ (half-Cauchy/half-Normal) rather than a moment estimator** | Gelman (2006), *Bayesian Analysis* 1(3) | [S] |
| **Imposing a common autoregressive coefficient on a heterogeneous dynamic panel is inconsistent as T→∞**, not merely biased — the deviation `(φ_i − φ̄)` is correlated with `y_{i,t−1}` by construction, so the correlation is structural rather than a finite-sample artifact. **Distinct from Nickell bias, which does vanish as T→∞.** The Mean Group estimator stays consistent; inconsistency is a pooling artifact. Primary paywalled — substance is standard, exact regularity conditions unverified | Pesaran & Smith (1995), *J. Econometrics* 68:79–113 | [S] / **[U]** on conditions |
| Pooled Mean Group estimator; Hausman test of PMG against MG | Pesaran, Shin & Smith (1999), *JASA* 94:621–634 | [S] |
| Fixed-effects dynamic panel bias ≈ **−(1+φ)/(T−1)** | Nickell (1981), *Econometrica* 49:1417–1426 | [S] |
| AR(1) small-sample bias **E[φ̂] − φ ≈ −(1+3φ)/T**, which **assumes a fitted intercept**. With a known or zero mean the leading bias is smaller (≈ −2φ/T). Primary (Biometrika 1954) unreachable; the exact constants are unconfirmed | Kendall (1954); Marriott & Pope (1954) | **[U]** |
| Half-life sensitivity `dh/dφ = ln2/[φ(lnφ)²]` diverges as φ→1. **At a 25-day half-life (φ = 0.9727), dh/dφ ≈ 927; SE(h)/h ≤ 20% needs T ≥ ~1,854 observations (~7.4 years)** — and 2–4× that in practice | delta method + `Var(φ̂) ≈ (1−φ²)/T` (Hamilton 1994) | [V] computed |
| Indirect inference preferred to first-order analytic corrections near a unit root | Gouriéroux, Monfort & Renault (1993), *J. Applied Econometrics* 8:S85–S118 | [S] |
| Swamy test: `S = Σ(β̂_i−β̄)'Σ̂_i⁻¹(β̂_i−β̄) ~ χ²(K(N−1))` — **and β̄ is a GLS/precision-weighted mean, `β̄ = [ΣΣ̂_i⁻¹]⁻¹ΣΣ̂_i⁻¹β̂_i`, not a simple average.** Getting that wrong inflates the statistic | Swamy (1970), *Econometrica* 38; Stata `xtrc` | [V] via textbook restatement |
| **Shrinkage beats both pooled and per-entity estimation out of sample, including where homogeneity is statistically rejected** | Baltagi & Griffin (1997), *J. Econometrics* 77; Baltagi, Bresson & Pirotte (2000) | [S] |
| Vasicek beta shrinkage `β_adj,i = w_i·β̂_i + (1−w_i)·β̄_cs`, `w_i = σ²_cs/(σ²_cs + se_i²)` — mathematically consistent with precision-weighted Bayesian updating. **But whether σ²_cs is the raw observed cross-sectional variance or bias-corrected for `E[se²]` could NOT be confirmed** (Wiley paywalled). This is the same trap as the τ² moment estimator; state which you use | Vasicek (1973), *J. Finance* 28:1233–1239 | **[U]** on σ²_cs |
| **"0.33 + 0.67·β" is a rounded practitioner convention** (widely attributed to a Merrill Lynch adjusted-beta service), **not a literal quote of Blume's regression coefficients**, which varied by sub-period | Blume (1971) | **[U]** |
| Bayes-Stein shrinkage: target `μ₀ = (1'Σ⁻¹μ̂)/(1'Σ⁻¹1)`, intensity `φ = (N+2)/[(N+2) + T(μ̂−μ₀1)'Σ⁻¹(μ̂−μ₀1)]`. Citation confirmed via the author's own CV page. **Whether Σ here is the plain sample covariance, and the form of Jorion's separate covariance adjustment, are unverified — do not implement that piece from memory** | Jorion (1986), *JFQA* 21:279–292 | **[U]** on Σ |
| **A single hierarchical fit is not multiple testing — but only if nothing is filtered on afterwards** | Gelman, Hill & Yajima (2012), *J. Research on Educational Effectiveness* 5:189–211 | [S] |
| False discovery rate control | Benjamini & Hochberg (1995), *JRSS-B* 57:289–300 | [V] |
| Regularised multi-task learning is structurally identical to a hierarchical Bayes prior; MAML is equivalent to hierarchical Bayes under a Laplace approximation | Evgeniou & Pontil (2004); Grant et al. (2018), ICLR | [S] |
| **SE(ŜR) ≈ √((1 + ½ŜR²)/T)** for IID returns, ŜR non-annualised | Lo (2002), *FAJ* 58(4) — confirmed via Bailey & López de Prado, who reproduce the derivation | [V] indirect |
| **The autocorrelation-corrected η(q) factor could NOT be verified.** General variance-ratio form is `η(q) = q/√(q + 2Σ(q−k)ρ_k)`, collapsing to √q under IID — but whether Lo gives a closed form for AR(1), and what it is, is unconfirmed. **Do not implement from memory; source the paper** | — | **[U]** |
| **MinTRL = 1 + [1 − γ̂₃·ŜR + (γ̂₄−1)/4·ŜR²]·(Z_α/(ŜR − SR*))²** — verified term for term. **Originates in "The Sharpe Ratio Efficient Frontier" (2012), not the DSR paper.** ŜR non-annualised; needs ~30+ obs before the moments are trustworthy | Bailey & López de Prado (2012), *Journal of Risk* | [V] read in full |
| Probability of backtest overfitting (CSCV) | Bailey, Borwein, López de Prado & Zhu (2014) | [S] |
| **DSR verified exactly:** `SR₀ = √V[{ŜR_n}]·[(1−γ)Z⁻¹(1−1/N) + γZ⁻¹(1−1/(Ne))]`, `DSR = Z[(ŜR−SR₀)√(T−1)/√(1 − γ̂₃ŜR + (γ̂₄−1)/4·ŜR²)]`, γ = 0.5772. **N is the number of *independent* trials** — our counter records trials, not independent ones; state the assumption | Bailey & López de Prado (2014), *JPM* 40(5) | [V] read in full |
| `IR = IC × TC × √BR`; **TC typically 0.3–0.8** for constrained portfolios | Clarke, de Silva & Thorley (2002), *FAJ* | [S] |
| Effective breadth collapses far below nominal position count when forecasts are cross-sectionally correlated | Qian & Hua (2004) | [S] |
| Bid-ask bounce drives much of measured short-horizon reversal; profits largely vanish measured off quote midpoints | short-horizon reversal literature (equity microstructure) | [S] |
| French & Roll (1986), *JFE* 17(1):5–26 — the classic trading-time vs non-trading-time variance reference. **Citation exact; the magnitude could not be read (paywalled), and no published overnight/intraday variance split for bond ETFs exists at all.** Which is why §9 computes it on our own data | French & Roll (1986) | [V] citation / **[U]** magnitude |
| **Corwin-Schultz verified against the authors' own distributed code and data description** (equations 14 and 18): `CONST = 3−2√2`; `β_t = [ln(H_t/L_t)]² + [ln(H_{t−1}/L_{t−1})]²`; `γ_t = [ln(H_max2day/L_min2day)]²`; `α_t = (√(2β)−√β)/CONST − √(γ/CONST)`; `S_t = 2(e^α−1)/(1+e^α)` | Corwin & Schultz (2012), *J. Finance* 67(2) | [V] |
| **Negative estimates are set to ZERO, not missing**, before averaging — the author's own 2014 note tests both and finds zero matches TAQ-quoted spreads better | Corwin, "Dealing with Negative Values" (2014) | [V] |

All three series equal `data/spreads_daily.parquet` on 7,715 common dates. On 07-28, 28 volumes differ, and LQDH's close moved from 92.2506 to 92.2510. Alpaca's SIP close that day is 92.2506. **`adj_close` differs in every cell**: Yahoo re-bases Adj Close at every distribution, and the new/old ratio is constant per ticker to ≤ 6e-6, between 0.982 and 0.998. <br>• **`returns_wide` and `close_unadj_wide`**: identical through 2026-07-27. That leg's total return is Adj Close `pct_change`, and the re-basing above moves it in its low digits. <br>**Yahoo's Adj Close is not stable even between two fetches.** Two complete rebuilds 30 minutes apart on 2026-10-11 differed only in `etf_panel.adj_close` (121,875 cells, ≤ 2e-6 relative, a few float32 steps) and in the yfinance-leg `ret_total` of `etf_daily` (3,061 cells, ≤ 0.009bp). Every other output, and every raw Close, OHLC, Volume and Dividends value, was byte-identical between the two runs. **So no series derived from Yahoo Adj Close can be reproduced bitwise, by any fetcher.** The in-house TR, (Close + distributions)/prev − 1, can be; `build_etf_daily.py`'s 2024 CRSP gate passes it for all 13 names (max 1.75bp, ANGL). ## 9. Internal measurements

Facts established on our own data rather than from a source. **These are
reproducible — the method is stated so they can be re-run and disputed.**

| claim | method | date |
|---|---|---|
| **Overnight share of close-to-close variance, 2019+: HYG 59.6%, JNK 55.0%, TLT 54.5%, LQD 48.6%, SPY 43.7%.** Majority of HYG's variance arrives overnight | `Σ[ln(O_t/C_{t−1})]² / Σ[ln(C_t/C_{t−1})]²` on `data/rv/etf_ohlc.parquet` | 2026-09-09 |
| Not a reversal artifact: overnight/intraday correlation near zero (HYG ρ = −0.046) | same panel | 2026-09-09 |
| Not a stale-open artifact: HYG opening gaps 2019+ have median 0.0bp, sd 44bp, 4.0% exactly zero, 9.7% beyond ±50bp | same panel | 2026-09-09 |
| **French & Roll still holds per unit of clock time** — HYG intraday variance intensity 0.55 vs 0.27 overnight (×10⁻⁴/day-equivalent). The overnight *total* is larger only because the window is ~17.5h against a 6.5h session | same panel | 2026-09-09 |
| **Parkinson's estimator recovers only ~48% of HYG's close-to-close variance since 2019** — the size of the benchmark error G3 Part C exists to prevent | same panel | 2026-09-09 |
| **Distribution carry on the live band's holdings path: −0.01%/yr full sample**, sd 1.23%/yr. By era: 2010–14 **−0.77%**, 2015–19 +0.32%, 2020–22 +0.36%, 2023–26 +0.25% | `Σ_i H.shift(2)_it · (D_it/P_i,t−1)` over 3,962 ex-dates, 2005–2026 | 2026-09-09 |
| **This withdraws an earlier estimate of "about 2%/yr"** made before measurement. The legs' yields net out; the book is not systematically long or short yield | — | 2026-09-09 |
| Per-fund κ identification budget: a 25-day half-life needs **T ≥ ~1,854 paired observations** for ±20% precision (φ = 0.9727, dh/dφ ≈ 927, SE(φ) ≤ 0.0054). **PDO has 1,407 and fails; DSL 3,362, BIT 3,402, PDI 3,592 are marginal** on the realistic 2–4× bound | delta method + `Var(φ̂) ≈ (1−φ²)/T` on the paired price/NAV panel | 2026-09-09 |
| `band_frontier.py::band()` **raises on a vector `b`**; `qualifyContracts` appears nowhere in `src/`; an option target without `meta["limit_price"]` becomes a $0.00 limit; `exec_ledger.py` has **zero** occurrences of expiry/assign/exercise | direct code read and execution | 2026-09-09 |
| **MQY and MHD are BlackRock funds, not Nuveen** — splitting the muni group across two leverage mechanisms | `cef_facts.csv` `longName` | 2026-09-09 |
| **`validate.py` scored `shift(1)` until 2026-09-10** — an entry at day *t*'s close on day *t*'s NAV, which publishes after it. Corrected: gross **1.27→0.94**, net **0.83→0.51**, walk-forward **8/9→7/9** (worst −0.45), bootstrap P(SR≤0) **0.000%→0.300%**, DSR@48 **0.870→0.333**. Reproduces the cost predicted on 2026-07-31 and never acted on | `python3 scripts/cef/validate.py --trials 48`, panel to 2026-09-09; stored verbatim in `pre-clean-slate:results/cef/EXECUTION_CONVENTION_2026-09-10.md` and `pre-clean-slate:results/cef/runs/` | 2026-09-10 |
| **The deflated Sharpe now FAILS at every trial count** — 0.588 @N=10, 0.333 @48, 0.330 @49, 0.197 @162. Observed 0.51 sits **below** the best-of-48 null of 0.60. The contract itself qualifies (HYG 20261016 P75, conId 823320930), so this is a data-service limit, not a bad contract. **`gamma/G2` cannot build an HYG surface from IBKR**, so the credit leg of the gamma programme has no data source | same run | 2026-09-12 |
| **Options permission is still UNMEASURED, and a read-only probe cannot answer it.** `whatIfOrder` returns `[]` — a timeout, not a refusal — because `readonly=True` is enforced at the gateway, which drops the order message; `ib_async.IB.placeOrder` has no readonly check of its own, so there is no exception to catch. **A timeout is not evidence of a refusal.** Answer it in the Client Portal rather than by re-probing without read-only | same run | 2026-09-12 |
| **Early exercise on a monthly-distributing credit ETF is NOT negligible — measured, where G1 only argued it.** `american_binomial` − `bs_discrete_div` at HYG parameters reaches **+9.5 implied-vol points** (35d put, 1.10 moneyness) and **+23.4 vol points** (21d call, 0.90 moneyness). **At the money it is small: 0.08–0.28 vol points.** So a European inversion is adequate ATM and badly wrong in the wings — which is exactly where a surface needs the wings | `python3 scripts/gamma/early_exercise_grid.py`; `pre-clean-slate:results/gamma/OPTION_MATH_2026-09-11.md` | 2026-09-11 |
| **The CRR lattice converges as a clean O(1/n), `gap × n = 0.640`** (ATM 60d call, σ=0.20; stable to three figures over n = 64…4,000, no sawtooth). **G1 Part C's "under 1e-4 at 2,000 steps" is not achievable with a plain CRR tree** — the law puts 1e-4 at n ≈ 6,400. The prompt's figure was an expectation, not a measurement | same run | 2026-09-11 |
| **Implied vol is NOT IDENTIFIED in the deep short-dated wings, and bisection there returns a confident wrong number.** A 7-day HYG call at 0.85 moneyness has a price identical to double precision for every σ from 0.01 to 0.156 — vega per vol point is exactly 0.0 — and an unguarded solver returned **0.156 for a true 0.06**. `optmath.implied_vol` now returns **None** there | `pre-clean-slate:src/deploy/lib/optmath.py::_is_identified`; `test_implied_vol_is_none_where_the_option_has_no_vega` | 2026-09-11 |
| **Three live defects on the option order path, not four and not eight.** A1 combo legs at `conId=0` (`qualifyContracts` was absent from `src/` entirely), A2 a missing `limit_price` becoming a **$0.00 limit order**, A3 a `MarketOrder` on a multi-leg option BAG. **G4 Part A's A4 was already fixed** (`exec_ledger.py:779` reads `.lib.odd_lot`; `src/deploy/v2` does not exist) and `G0` §5 / `G2` Part D still assert it is live. The multiplier and `leg.ratio` items an audit added are **not defects** — both sit behind `kind != OPTION` guards, and IB combo semantics are `ratio × package qty` | `src/deploy/tests/test_option_order_path.py`, each fix verified to fail before it was kept | 2026-09-11 |
| **C3, the CEF stress nowcast, DOES NOT CLEAR `G6`'s bar — it fails the holdout.** Full sample it looks good: beside C1, β **+0.0097**, t **+2.42**, incremental R² **+0.0358**. It also survives the mechanical control — against **forward realised vol with no IV term at all**, β **+0.02105**, t **+3.95**, n=4,851 — so CEF stress genuinely does carry information about HYG's next week. **But on the 2023–2026 holdout the coefficient falls to 24% of its full-sample value** (+0.02085 → +0.00490), t drops to **+1.69**, and on the stated target it is **−0.20**. G6 requires full sample AND holdout | `python3 scripts/gamma/conditioners.py`; `pre-clean-slate:results/gamma/CONDITIONERS_2026-09-13.md` | 2026-09-13 |
| **A trap in `G6` Part B's own target specification.** `RV_fwd − IV_t` contains IV_t, and C1 is built from IV_t (corr **+0.272**), so part of any fit on that target is arithmetic rather than prediction — C1's β is **negative** (−0.0092, t −2.11) largely for that reason. **Regress on `RV_fwd` alone to separate them.** C3 passes that control; the reported incremental R² on the stated target overstates the real effect | same run | 2026-09-13 |
| **C2 (variance term structure) is DECLARED-NOT-RUN, recorded before results** per G6's "declare all three and do not amend after seeing results". It needs a 1m/3m ATM ratio, i.e. a surface, and IBKR serves no historical option bars. Unavailable, not unpromising | same run | 2026-09-13 |
| **THE CREDIT VARIANCE RISK PREMIUM, measured — `W14 Part A` had to record this UNMEASURED.** HYG implied (VXHYG) against subsequent 21-session close-to-close realised, 2015-04-22 → 2026-07-30, n=1,862: **median 2.99x in variance, ~1.73x in vol**. On medians of the levels: implied **7.78%** against realised **4.68%**. | `python3 scripts/gamma/credit_base_rate.py`; `pre-clean-slate:results/gamma/CREDIT_BASE_RATE_2026-09-13.md` | 2026-09-12 |
| **Credit gamma is MORE THAN TWICE as expensive as equity gamma**, on the identical construction: variance ratio **2.77x (HYG)** against **1.28x (SPY)**; SPY implied 14.02% vs realised 12.40%. A credit ETF's implied vol prices jump/default risk that realised almost never delivers. **This is the single largest headwind the gamma programme faces and it was unmeasured before today** | same run; SPY leg from `data/vrp/vrp_series.parquet` | 2026-09-12 |
| **Long gamma in HYG would have paid on only 9.1% of days** (implied < subsequent realised). By era: 2015–19 **10.0%**, 2020–22 **11.5%**, **2023–26 just 1.8%**. That is the base rate any `G6` conditioner must beat — stated before C1 or C3 was run | same run | 2026-09-12 |
| **CORRECTION — `G0_BRIEF` §2 mislabels the repo's own SPY column.** It describes `rich_ratio` as "the ratio of implied to **forward** realised variance ... median 1.32, mean 1.55". Reverse-engineered exactly against `data/vrp/vrp_series.parquet`, **`rich_ratio == impl_var / rv_TRAIL`** (max error 0.0; medians 1.3011 / 1.5253 reproduce). Trailing is an ex-ante rich/cheap INDICATOR and is what `C1` can condition on; forward is the PREMIUM itself and is only knowable after the fact. Not the same quantity | `pre-clean-slate:scripts/gamma/credit_base_rate.py` docstring | 2026-09-12 |
| **The CEF book is NOT short volatility.** β on ΔVIX **−0.000092/pt (t −0.69)** in the E1 §5 factor set, NW(5), n=2,742 — **$−46 per VIX point** on the $500k book. Univariate it is −0.000343 (t −2.80), so what ΔVIX picks up alone is already carried by the credit and equity legs. Dropping the straddle leg to recover the book's first year gives −0.000247 (t −1.93, n=3,291) — the least favourable window, still short of the |t| ≥ 2.5 bar | `python3 scripts/cef/stress_beta.py`; `pre-clean-slate:results/cef/STRESS_BETA_2026-09-11.md` | 2026-09-11 |
| **The verdict is scoped to the gate, not broader.** `SHORTVOL` — the only *tradeable* vol factor, a short-ATM-straddle return — loads **+0.105 (t +1.34)** full sample and **+0.190 (t +1.90)** on the 2023-26 holdout, which is the short-vol sign; univariate ΔVIX (−0.000343, t −2.80) clears |t| ≥ 2.5 alone. The claim is **"a mild short-vol exposure that an option cannot profitably hedge"**, NOT "no vol exposure" | same run | 2026-09-11 |
| **0 of 10 worst non-overlapping 21-session windows START in the top VIX quintile**; 6 of 10 contain one. The two worst begin at the 1st and 11th VIX percentiles. **W14's Part B gate FAILS on both conditions — the convexity overlay is closed, 0 trials spent** | same run | 2026-09-11 |
| **The drawdowns are single-name events, not market events.** Across the ten worst windows one fund is a median **60%** of the loss; PHK leads 3, DSL 3, PTY 2. In Feb–Mar 2020 the book lost 3.98% while **PHK alone lost 8.37%** — the other 16 names made **+4.39%** | same run | 2026-09-11 |
| **The signal is 2.39× stronger in stress.** IC by VIX tercile at signal time: low **−0.0392** (t −2.88), mid −0.0417, high **−0.0936** (t −6.27). Deciding on a top-decile VIX day (threshold 26.07 **within the traded sample**), the 21 sessions the decision earns — *t+2*..*t+22* under shift(2) — return **+1.60%** against +0.84% unconditionally, 330 entries. A hedge would protect the P&L the strategy exists to earn | same run | 2026-09-11 |
| **The book's exposure to HYG is R² = 0.0126** on daily excess returns (3,291 days) — the ceiling on how much of its variance any HYG instrument can address. On one common sample (n = 2,742) HYG is the **weakest** of four candidate regressors: HYG 0.0155, short-straddle proxy 0.0190, SPY 0.0246, ΔVIX 0.0289. **The only credit-volatility instrument this account can reach explains less of the book than the equity index does** | same run | 2026-09-11 |
| **The live band holds NOTHING before 2013-06-14** — 2,126 of 5,456 panel sessions are exactly flat, not merely thin. Landmine 6's dilution checks out: 1.209 ÷ √(3,330/5,456) = 1.547 against 1.550 measured on traded sessions only. **This book has never traded a credit crisis**, so every era and drawdown claim about it is a statement about 2013–2026 | same run | 2026-09-11 |
| **Selection within groups pays more per unit of signal than timing between them** (W10 Part B, fit period). Deployed set: pooled slope within **−9.07bp/z (t −3.02)**, between **−3.29 (t −0.87)**; IC within −0.051, between +0.007. WIDE 13-name set (context): within −12.58 (t −9.10), between −9.07 (t −5.21), and since 2013 −9.96 against −4.48 (difference t 2.46). The deployed P&L splits exactly into within, 89% of live gross return (SR 1.03), and between, 11% (group spread alone 0.03%/yr) | `python3 scripts/cef/w10_partb.py`; `results/cef/W10_PARTB_2026-10-12.md` | 2026-10-12 |
| **The deployed 13-name book existed on only 43.6% of sessions from 2013-06 to 2022-12.** On the rest fewer than `min_names` cleared the ADV floor and `build_targets` held the previous target, sometimes for six months. On the NYSE opening-auction print (2016–22): session −0.046 (t −2.85). The [opening auction t+1 → close t+2] window: −0.073, against the deployed window's −0.032 on the same dates. t's NAV move predicts the t+1 session (−99bp/unit, t −2.57); t's price move does not | `python3 scripts/cef/core_signal_decomp.py t1`; `results/cef/CORE_SIGNAL_CANDIDATES_2026-10-12.md` §1 | 2026-10-12 |

## 10. Open questions — status 2026-09-10

### Closed by derivation (the papers were not needed)

Three formulas were flagged "must be re-sourced before coding". All three are
now settled without the paywalled originals, and the derivations are in the
prompts that use them:

- **Lo's η(q) autocorrelation factor** — derived from the variance of a q-period
  sum, with the AR(1) closed form
  `Σ_{k=1}^{q−1}(q−k)ρ^k = [qρ(1−ρ) − ρ(1−ρ^q)]/(1−ρ)²`. Verified against
  brute-force summation to machine precision and against a Monte Carlo on
  simulated AR(1) returns. `η(q,0) = √q` exactly. **→ W1**
- **Whalley-Wilmott `S` vs `S²`** — settled by dimensional analysis: Γ carries
  price⁻¹, λ carries price⁻¹, so the bracket is dimensionless only at `S¹`.
  **The desk's original spec was right; the only real error was the γ/Γ notation
  collision.** The (3/2) leading constant remains uncited and does not matter,
  since G3 uses only the `Γ^(2/3)` scaling. **→ G3**
- **Vasicek's σ²_cs / the τ² question** — settled by the law of total variance:
  `Var(θ̂ across i) = E[se²] + τ²`, so the numerator is **always** the
  bias-corrected quantity. What Vasicek actually wrote is irrelevant to what is
  correct. Simulated at N = 17 over 4,000 draws to show when it matters. **→ F2**

### Still open, in priority order

1. **NYSE Arca's closing-auction cutoff and imbalance threshold.** Arca lists
   HYG, LQD and SPY — everything the gamma hedging touches. Attempted again
   2026-09-10: nyse.com's Arca rulebook PDF 404s, and EDGAR full-text search
   returns zero hits for the Rule 7.35-E closing-auction timing language.
   **This is a broker/rulebook question, not a research question** — ask IBKR or
   read the rulebook through a paid SRO service. G3 already gates the first live
   hedge on it.
2. **DSL's option depth on a live broker chain.** Public data says real but
   shallow (most strikes 0–6 OI; best line 83 OI at a ~22% spread). Only an IBKR
   chain settles whether one ATM point is usable.
3. **IBKR's actual recall terms and notice practice** — contractual under the
   MSLA, not Reg SHO. Ask; record with a date.
4. **Whether HYG's price-vs-NAV basis noise is predictable** — the one genuinely
   open *research* question (G2 Part E). A hypothesis with a measurement
   attached, not a gap.
5. **The Aragon/Chen/Shi ETF variance-swap figure**, if anyone gets SSRN access.
   HYG-specific evidence would be valuable; Carr & Wu covers indices and single
   names only. Not blocking — the claim it supported is now made better by
   Carr & Wu.
6. **Magnitudes where the direction is already confirmed and nothing depends on
   the size:** Starks/Yong/Zheng, Khorana/Wahal/Zenner, Simon & Campasano,
   Ji & Kim's tables, Boyle & Emanuel's reported numbers. G3 verifies the 1/N
   hedging-error scaling on our own path instead of citing it.
7. **CEF bid-ask spreads and closing-auction share of volume.** No public
   dataset exists; W7 measures them from our own fills.

### Genuine negatives — findings, not gaps

- **No study anywhere estimates CEF discount half-life at daily or weekly
  frequency.** Every locatable paper is monthly at best, 4–8 month half-lives,
  against our 24.6-day implied figure.
- **Bradley et al. (2010) contains no short-window announcement CAR** and
  reports no completion rate.
- **No published overnight/intraday variance decomposition exists for bond
  ETFs**, which is why §9's own measurement is the only source we have.

### Corrections made to this file's own earlier entries

Seven claims recorded on 2026-09-09 were wrong and are fixed above: the BTZ
quarterly R² (6.82%, not "teens"); the SCOTUS vote (6–3, not unanimous); Bradley
et al. on open-ending (conversion *eliminates* the discount — the "half" applies
to funds that did **not** convert); the NYSE imbalance (published once, not
refreshing); the rights-offering price (95% formula, 90% NAV floor); the recall
notice period (contractual, not Reg SHO); and the Aragon −74% figure (withdrawn
as unverifiable).

### A fetch artifact that was never a source (moved 2026-09-10, W0c §4)

`wu_paper_archive.html` sat at the **repo root**, beside `README.md` and
`CLAUDE.md`, named as though it were an archived copy of a Wu paper. **It is
not.** Opened and read 2026-09-10: it is a 4.6 KB Internet Archive error page
whose entire content is *"The Wayback Machine has not archived that URL"* for
`http://faculty.baruch.cuny.edu/lwu/` (Liuren Wu's faculty page). It contains no
paper, no abstract and no figure, and **nothing in the repo ever cited it**.

It is now `pre-clean-slate:results/ops/wu_faculty_page_wayback_MISS_2026-09-09.html` — renamed,
not deleted, because the failed fetch is itself a small piece of evidence: it
records that the archive route to that page was tried on 2026-09-09 and returned
nothing. **A file whose name asserts a provenance it does not have is worse than
no file**, which is the only reason this note exists rather than a silent move.

The Carr & Wu (2009) and Wang, Zhou & Zhou (2011) entries above are unaffected —
both were read in full from their own primary sources and are marked `[V]`.

by a deep-research workflow on 2026-10-08. Each claim marked [V] was put to three
adversarial verifiers, who fetched the source in that session; two refutes out of
three killed a claim. **The note's author did not re-read them.** "Abstract"
means only the abstract was reachable. Search-phase leads that never went to the
vote are [S].

|---|---|---|

No lock-out duration is stated. The client spaces requests 0.125s apart (8/s), one at a time, and backs off 30/120/600s on 403/429/503, then raises | https://www.sec.gov/os/accessing-edgar-data ("Last Reviewed or Updated: June 26, 2024"), fetched 2026-10-12 | [V] |

### 12i. The XASSET slate: published parameters (added 2026-10-12)

The sources behind `results/xasset/PREREG_XASSET_SLATE_2026-10-12.md` (plan
gate G8). **Each was fetched and read in full on 2026-10-12 by the author of
that draft**, and every parameter the draft takes from it is quoted with its
page. Page numbers are the journal's printed pages where a journal PDF was
read, otherwise the working paper's. The PDFs were saved to session scratch
only (the repository is public and holds no third-party PDF).

| claim / parameter | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| **TSMOM rule.** Ex-ante variance σ²ₜ = 261 Σᵢ (1−δ)δⁱ (r₍ₜ₋₁₋ᵢ₎ − r̄ₜ)², δ chosen so that the centre of mass δ/(1−δ) = **60 days**, "the volatility estimates at time t−1 applied to time-t returns" (§2.4, eq. 1, p.233). "The 12-month time series momentum strategy with a 1-month holding period (k=12 and h=1)"; position size **40%/σₜ₋₁**, "The choice of 40% is inconsequential"; r^TSMOM = sign(r₍ₜ₋₁₂,ₜ₎)·(40%/σₜ₋₁)·r₍ₜ,ₜ₊₁₎ (§4, §4.1, eq. 5, p.236); the diversified factor is the equal-weighted average over the Sₜ securities available at t (p.236). Evaluation starts in 1985 "to ensure that a comprehensive set of instruments have data" (p.236). Returns are daily excess returns of the most liquid contract (§2.1, p.231). Speculators "earn positive excess returns at the expense of hedgers, who may be willing to compensate speculators for liquidity provision in order to maintain their hedge" (p.249) | Moskowitz, Ooi & Pedersen (2012), *JFE* 104(2):228–250, https://pages.stern.nyu.edu/~lpederse/papers/TimeSeriesMomentum.pdf (the published Elsevier PDF) | [V] full text |
| **TSH control.** "r^TSH,i₍ₜ₊₁₎ = sign(r^i₍₁,ₜ₎) r^i₍ₜ₊₁₎, where r^i₍₁,ₜ₎ is the accumulative return of asset i from month 1 to month t" (§5.1, eq. 17, p.788). Buys if the historical sample mean is non-negative, sells if negative (Table 9 caption, p.789; Table 10 caption, p.791). Returns are excess returns throughout (§2, p.776). Portfolio level, 1986–2015: equal weighting, mean difference TSM−TSH **0.14%/month, p 0.19** (§5.2, p.790); **volatility weighting as in MOP, (1/N)·40%/σ: mean difference 0.42%/month, p 0.07; FF4 alpha difference 0.32, p 0.08; AMP three-factor alpha difference 0.10, p 0.56** (Table 10 Panel B, p.791). Both strategies' profits come mainly from their long legs (p.790) | Huang, Li, Wang & Zhou (2020), *JFE* 135(3):774–794; the published PDF reached through the CEMA WP 717 link on IDEAS, https://down.aefweb.net/WorkingPapers/w717.pdf (SSRN and the SMU repository refused automated fetches) | [V] full text |
| **Rebalancing signals.** A simulated 60/40 S&P 500 / 10-year Treasury portfolio, weights updated wₜ₊₁ = wₜ(1+R^SP)/[wₜ(1+R^SP) + (1−wₜ)(1+R^10Y)] (p.10; App. B, p.53). Threshold: rebalance when the equity weight is more than δ from 60%; the signal is the drifted weight minus 60% (eq. B.1, p.53). **Threshold Signal = the average over δ from 0% to 2.5% in steps of 0.1%** (eq. 2, p.12). Calendar: rebalance on the last business day of each month (p.9; eq. B.2, p.53). **week4 = the last 5 days of the month** (Dummy^5Days, p.14). **Strategy: R = (R^SP − R^10y)·w, w = the average of −Threshold/1.5% and a modified Calendar signal equal to sign(−Calendar) in the last week of the month, sign(Calendar₋₄) on the first business day of the new month, and zero otherwise** (§4, pp.37–38). The subscript "−4" is not defined further in the text **[U] reading**. Nor does the text say which sessions each Calendar component earns; the pre-registration's revision 3 (2026-10-13) declares week4 → E−3 … D1 and Calendar₋₄ → D2, from "set at s, earns s+1" **[U] reading**. Data: E-mini S&P 500 and 10-year note futures, 1997-09-10 → 2023-03-17 (App. A, p.52). Table 6 (p.38): excess return 10.20%/yr, vol 9.17%, Sharpe 1.11, skew 5.23; CAPM/C4/FF5/HXZ alphas 9.43–9.64%/yr; 17.96%/yr in high and 3.79%/yr in low liquidity-risk periods. Sharpe 0.90 excluding Sept 2008–Mar 2009 and Mar 2020 (p.39). Cumulative price-pressure coefficients trough at day 4 (Threshold) and day 2 (Calendar) and revert within about 15 days (Fig. 4, pp.21–22). Rebalancing costs "about $16 billion annually" (abstract; p.3). **δ ≈ 2pp and the month-end window were posited from institutional practice and then confirmed on the authors' own sweeps (Figs. 2–3); footnote 10 (p.12) reports a first-half-sample re-estimate** | Harvey, Mazzoleni & Melone, "The Unintended Consequences of Rebalancing", Dice Center WP 2025-01, version of 2026-01-14, https://www.edhec.edu/sites/default/files/2026-03/Scientific%20paper.%20ssrn-5122748.pdf | [V] full text |
| **Month-end Treasury strategy.** Buy the security "t trading days before the end of the month, and selling it on the last day of the month (at the close of business)", financed at GC repo, on the Gürkaynak–Sack–Wright off-the-run zero curve (§2.1, p.4). "Investing in the 10-year note over the last 3 days of the month gives an average excess return of 0.25 percent per month" (p.5; Table 1, p.25, SE 0.05); excess returns largest over the last 3–5 days (p.5); Sharpe best over the final 2–3 days (p.7); **10-year, t=3 annualised Sharpe 0.88** (Table 3, p.26); all other days 0.07%/month (SE 0.13), Sharpe 0.11 (Tables 1, 3). Table 4 (p.27): 10-year, t=3, 2015–2019 window **0.27 (SE 0.10)**; the text calls the last window 2015–2018 (p.8). The Bloomberg Barclays US Treasury Index rebalances on the final trading day of each month; life insurers buy the securities added to it on that day (§5.2, pp.18–20). "The entire term premium is concentrated in the last few days of the month" (p.21) | Hartley & Schwarz, "Predictable End-of-Month Treasury Returns" (2019-11-22), https://rodneywhitecenter.wharton.upenn.edu/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/17-19.Schwarz.pdf | [V] full text |
| Benchmark Treasury volume on the last trading day of the month is about 46% above the same weekday in the surrounding two weeks since 2020 (51% excluding December); price impact about 28% lower. The authors "conjecture that increased turn-of-month portfolio rebalancing by passive investment funds that manage relative to fixed-income indices helps explain these patterns". **Revised 2025-10-08 for a coding error ("qualitatively the same and somewhat stronger quantitatively")**. Volume and liquidity only, not returns | Dyer, Fleming & Shachar, "End-of-Month Liquidity in the Treasury Market", Liberty Street Economics, 2024-09-24, https://libertystreeteconomics.newyorkfed.org/2024/09/end-of-month-liquidity-in-the-treasury-market/ | [V] page read through WebFetch |
| **Cross-sectional momentum, AMP form.** MOM2–12, "the past 12-month cumulative raw return on the asset ... skipping the most recent month's return", the same measure for every asset class (§I.B, pp.936–937). Factor weights wᵢₜ = cₜ(rank(Sᵢₜ) − Σᵢ rank(Sᵢₜ)/N), scaled to one dollar long and one dollar short (§I.D, eq. 1, p.938). "Weighting the nonstock asset classes by their ex ante volatility gives similar results" (fn. 10, p.938). Government bonds: 10-year bonds of 10 countries | Asness, Moskowitz & Pedersen (2013), *J. Finance* 68(3), https://pages.stern.nyu.edu/~lpederse/papers/ValMomEverywhere.pdf | [V] full text |
| **Cross-sectional momentum, BSV form.** Trend and momentum are "the 12-month-minus-1-month excess return", skipping the last month (App. A.I, p.52). Cross-sectional weights wᵢₜ = zₜ(Rank(Sᵢₜ) − (Nₜ+1)/2), at least two markets; each position then sized by **a simple 3-year rolling volatility estimate on monthly data, floored at the larger of the 10% quantile of the asset class's estimates and 2.5%**; each factor then scaled with a 10-year rolling window to 10% ex-ante volatility; monthly rebalancing (App. A.II, pp.54–55). Bonds = 10-year government bond indices. Sharpe ratios, replication 1981–2011 (Table II, p.42): **bond trend 0.53 (t 2.94); bond momentum 0.29 (t 1.61); multi-asset trend 1.09 (t 6.05)**. New sample 1800–1980 and 2012–16 (Table IV Panel A, p.44): 0.49 (t 7.22), 0.31 (t 4.56), 0.98 (t 14.43). Equity trend 0.62 / 0.75; commodity trend 0.88 / 0.23. **"The trend factors subsume the momentum factors ... trend encompasses momentum"** (spanning regressions, p.23) | Baltussen, Swinkels & van Vliet, "Global Factor Premiums" (WP 2019-01), https://www.institutional-investment.de/uploads/media/Robeco_Study_Global-Factor-Premiums.pdf; published *JFE* 142(3) (2021) | [V] WP full text; published figures may differ |
| **The slate's feasibility snapshot.** For each of the 19 instruments, from the in-house panels: first and last non-missing `ret_total` date, interior gaps against SPY's session calendar (**0 for all 19**), and the CRSP-panel first date. From CRSP `dsf` through the R2 mirror (`--crsp`): PERMNO (share code 73), first and last row, row count, NULL-`ret` count, negative-`prc` count and zero-volume count. **Every instrument has CRSP coverage to 2024-12-31, with exactly one NULL `ret` (its first day). EMB and SPY have one no-closing-trade day each (`prc` < 0) and one zero-volume day each; the other 17 have none.** The PERMNOs CRSP resolves for the 10 names already in `scripts/build_etf_daily.py` equal its `PERMNOS` map, with the same row counts. The other nine: SPY 84398, EMB 92491, PFF 91874, MUB 92273, BKLN 12631, CWB 92914, IEI 91705, TLH 91704, AGG 89848. Alpaca `name`, `status`, `exchange`, `tradable`, `shortable`, `easy_to_borrow`, `borrow_status`, read 2026-10-12T22:42:40–41Z on the cef paper key. **All 19 names are tradable; all are shortable and easy to borrow except MUB (not shortable, hard to borrow). None is NYSE-listed (ARCA or NASDAQ)**, so none can yet be scored on the official closing-auction print (`ASSET_EXCHANGE_SIP_NAME` maps NYSE only; plan item 0.2). Flags are true on their date only. Per test, arithmetic on the bar only (no return is computed): the first decision date under the declared breadth rule, the fit-period sessions before 2023-01-01, the Sharpe the bar demands (`validate._dsr` inverted), and the power at the published Sharpe. **Corrected 2026-10-13 (pre-registration revision 3, after an adversarial review):** T is now the P&L session count, from the first decision + 2 sessions (one fewer than before); the power SE is `_dsr`'s own denominator, not 1/√years; power is also stated at the draft's own expected Sharpes, the published figure becoming an upper reference (X2 at 0.2 / 0.5 / 0.8: P(pass) 0.006 / 0.118 / 0.566; at the published in-sample 1.11: 0.941); and the D4 column uses the binding post-2007 window (IR 0.76 for X2, X3 and X4, not 0.67 / 0.73). The script also prints the X5 ladder's first decision (2005-07-29), the X2 control's window from BIL's first return (3,926 sessions) and the fixed holdout to 2026-09-30 (939 sessions). The 2026-10-12 figures it replaces are in the pre-registration at `af61e28` | `python3 scripts/xasset/slate_feasibility.py --alpaca --crsp --factors` → `results/xasset/slate_feasibility_2026-10-12.csv`; the bar and power block re-run 2026-10-13 without flags | [V] |
| **CRSP no-closing-trade rows in the 13 tickers of `data/etf_daily.parquet`** (`dsf.prc` < 0, the bid/ask midpoint CRSP stores when no trade closed): ANGL 30, FALN 38, JNK 1; BIL, DBC, EEM, EFA, GLD, HYG, IEF, LQD, SHY, TLT none. | **AQR factor files.** "Time Series Momentum: Factors, Monthly": columns TSMOM, TSMOM^CM, ^EQ, ^FI, ^FX; 497 months, 1985-01-31 → 2026-05-29; "an updated and extended version of the factors used in" Moskowitz, Ooi & Pedersen (2012). "Value and Momentum Everywhere: Factors, Monthly": VAL, MOM, VAL^AA, MOM^AA and others; 655 months, 1972-01-31 → 2026-07-31. The file says "AQR reconstructs the full history each time the returns are updated", so every run must record the vintage it used. These are the reported spanning factors of the XASSET slate. **A fetcher (`scripts/data/fetch_aqr_factors.py`, writing to a gitignored cache) must be written before any run uses them.** Nothing fetched is committed | https://www.aqr.com/-/media/AQR/Documents/Insights/Data-Sets/Time-Series-Momentum-Factors-Monthly.xlsx and …/Value-and-Momentum-Everywhere-Factors-Monthly.xlsx; reachable and parsed for coverage only, `slate_feasibility.py --factors`, 2026-10-12 | [V] coverage |
| **AQR factor fetcher (XASSET gate DG2, 2026-10-14).** `scripts/data/fetch_aqr_factors.py` fetches the two AQR files above and writes `results/xasset/_cache_xasset/aqr_{tsmom,vme}_monthly.parquet` (gitignored; nothing fetched is committed). The parser locates the header row by its exact contents, requires the declared columns in order, requires consecutive month-ends, and raises on a non-numeric cell or on a monthly \|return\| > 1 (a unit change). Empty cells stay NaN (AQR publishes some sub-factors over shorter spans). **The files carry no as-of date, so each cached row records the vintage:** the downloaded file's SHA-256, the HTTP `Last-Modified` header, the UTC fetch time and the last data month. Fetched 2026-10-14: TSMOM 497 months 1985-01-31 → 2026-05-29, `Last-Modified` 2026-06-26; VME 655 months 1972-01-31 → 2026-07-31, `Last-Modified` 2026-09-23 | the AQR URLs above; `python3 scripts/data/fetch_aqr_factors.py` | [V] fetched and parsed 2026-10-14 |

### 12j. Regulatory plumbing for the event-driven layer (added 2026-10-12)

Fetched 2026-10-12 for `results/cef/EVENT_DRIVEN_POD_2026-10-12.md`. eCFR text
through its versioner API (point-in-time 2026-09-01); statutes from
law.cornell.edu.

| claim | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| Reg M Rule 105 covers "an offering of equity securities for cash pursuant to a registration statement" or Form 1-A/1-E. It bars buying the offered securities *from an underwriter or participating broker-dealer* after a short sale in the restricted period: the shorter of 5 business days before pricing and filing-to-pricing. **105(c): "This section shall not apply to offerings that are not conducted on a firm commitment basis."** There is no exclusion naming rights offerings, ATMs or closed-end funds; whether one is covered turns on firm commitment. 105(b)(3) excepts only *registered* funds buying where an affiliated fund or another series sold short | 17 CFR 242.105 | [V] |
| ICA §12(d)(1)(A): 3% of voting stock, 5% of the acquirer's assets in one fund, 10% in all funds. It reaches "any investment company" buying a *registered* fund. **§3(c)(1) and §3(c)(7)(D) funds are deemed investment companies only for (A)(i) and (B)(i)**: the 3% voting-stock cap binds a private fund; the 5% and 10% asset tests do not | 15 U.S.C. 80a-12(d)(1), 80a-3(c)(1), (c)(7)(D) | [V] statute |
| Rule 12d1-4 exempts only an acquiring fund that is a *registered investment company or a BDC*. A private fund cannot rely on it, so a §3(c)(1)/(7) acquirer stays at 3% of a listed CEF. "Private fund" appears in the rule only to limit the *acquired* fund's own holdings to 10% | 17 CFR 270.12d1-4 | [V] |
| Reg SHO 201(b)(1): the trigger is a price fall of **10% or more from the listing market's prior-day close**. Short orders may then not execute or display at or below the current national best bid, **for the rest of the day and the following day**. The duty sits on trading centres. Rule 203(b)(1): a broker-dealer may not accept or effect a short sale without borrowing, arranging to borrow, or reasonable grounds to believe the security can be borrowed, and it must document this | 17 CFR 242.201, 242.203 | [V] |
| 13D: more than 5%, filed **within 5 business days** of the acquisition. Amendments are due **within 2 business days**, and a 1% change is deemed material. 13G: passive filers under 13d-1(c) file **within 5 business days**. QIIs under 13d-1(b) and exempt investors under 13d-1(d) file **within 45 days after quarter-end** (QIIs: 5 business days after month-end if over 10%). A 13G amendment is due 45 days after quarter-end; a (c) filer also amends within 2 business days of crossing 10% and of each 5% change. **"Business day" excludes Federal holidays**, so Columbus and Veterans Day stop the clock while NYSE trades. Registered CEF shares are in scope by name (13d-1(i)(1)) | 17 CFR 240.13d-1, 240.13d-2 | [V] |
| EDGAR filing date: a filing whose transmission *commences* after 5:30 p.m. ET is deemed filed the next business day. 10 p.m. applies to Rule 462(b) filings and to Forms 3/4/5, 144, Schedule 14N and **Schedules 13D/13G**. Before the 2023 amendments, 13G filings after 5:30 p.m. took the next day (HYT 2007 and 2017 13Gs) | 17 CFR 232.13(a); data.sec.gov HYT submissions | [V] |
| `data.sec.gov/submissions` carries `acceptanceDateTime` per filing **in UTC** ("…Z"). The filing index page shows the same instant in ET: HYT Form 3 0001193125-26-394556 is `2026-09-17T21:31:47.000Z` in JSON and "Accepted 2026-09-17 17:31:47" on the index. 898 of 907 HYT filings fit UTC plus the cut-offs; the 9 exceptions are pre-2023 13Gs, CORRESP, EFFECT and 40-17G. The API updates with a "typical processing delay of less than a second". *(Corrected 2026-10-15, §12y: true for HYT, not for every registrant. For 6 of the 13 deployed names (DSL, JFR, MHD, MQY, PDI, PHK) every one of the 20 latest filings has a JSON value later than the filing's own SEC header by exactly that date's UTC offset (4 h EDT, 5 h EST); 120 of 260 filings overall. Take `observed_at` from the header's `<ACCEPTANCE-DATETIME>` (Eastern time), not the JSON.)* *(Also corrected 2026-10-15, §12z: the API time is one UTC offset late on 23 of 50 sampled filings; the header, not the API, is the acceptance time.)* | data.sec.gov; sec.gov EDGAR API page | [V]; the "UTC" rule [V] for HYT only |
| SEC fair access: "Current max request rate: 10 requests/second"; declare a User-Agent such as "Sample Company Name AdminContact@<sample company domain>.com". EDGAR accepts filings 6:00 a.m.–10:00 p.m. ET on business days | sec.gov "Accessing EDGAR Data" | [V] |
| SIFMA US 2026 (issued 2025-12-17; covers Treasuries, agency MBS/ABS, OTC IG/HY corporates and **municipal bonds**). Holidays listed: Jan 1, Jan 19, Feb 16, May 25, Jun 19, Jul 3, Sep 7, **Oct 12 (Columbus Day)**, **Nov 11 (Veterans Day)**, Nov 26, Dec 25, and Jan 1 2027. Early closes at 2:00 p.m. ET: May 22, Jul 2, Nov 27, Dec 24, Dec 31 (and 2025-12-31). **Good Friday, Apr 3, is a 12:00 p.m. early close, not a full close.** These are "recommendations only" | sifma.org holiday schedule and 2025-12-17 press release | [V] |
| NYSE 2026 holidays: Jan 1, Jan 19, Feb 16, Apr 3, May 25, Jun 19, Jul 3, Sep 7, Nov 26, Dec 25. **Columbus and Veterans Day are not on it, so NYSE trades.** Early closes at 1:00 p.m.: Nov 27 and Dec 24 (bonds close at 2:00 p.m. on those days) | nyse.com/markets/hours-calendars | [V] |
| Rule 13e-4(f): an issuer tender must stay open **at least 20 business days**, and 10 business days after any change in price or amount sought. Interval-fund repurchases under Rule 23c-3 are excluded (13e-4(h)(7)). The **2026-04-16 Corp Fin exemptive order** allows a 10-business-day minimum, but only for offers of "cash at a fixed price", so a tender at NAV on expiry appears not to qualify (inference) | 17 CFR 240.13e-4; sec.gov exemptive order 2026-04-16 | [V] text; applicability [U] |

All are "not available in paper trading". Locates are single-use and non-refundable. The daily HTB fee is Σ(HTB short MV × HTB rate)/360, charged in round lots, and a Friday position pays for 3 days. ETB shorts pay "$0 locate and borrow fees" for Trading API users. **No documented endpoint publishes a per-symbol annual HTB rate.** The asset object carries only `shortable`, `easy_to_borrow` and `borrow_status` | docs.alpaca.markets margin-and-short-selling (updated 2026-09-17); listlocates, createlocates, listlocatequotes (2026-06-19); get-v2-assets-1 | [V] |

Elite accounts get "up to 50%" of the interest; the non-Elite example implies 20%. Lent shares lose their vote. A payment in lieu replaces the dividend and may be taxed differently. Collateral is held at BMO. Close-outs: if Alpaca cannot borrow by T+1, it closes out on T+2 at about 9:30 ET; lenders can recall before 14:45 ET | Alpaca blog, 2025-07-29; docs fully-paid-securities-lending (updated 2026-08-31) | [V] |

Common events: new, fill, partial_fill, canceled, expired, done_for_day, replaced. Fill events carry price, qty and position_qty. Paper gives random partial fills 10% of the time. The trade-events SSE is a Broker API endpoint | docs websocket-streaming (2025-09-24); paper-trading | [V] docs; [U] in practice, because nothing in `quantt/` subscribes yet |

### 12o. Closing-auction capacity and the opening cross (added 2026-10-13)

The sources behind `results/cef/EXEC_CAPACITY_OPEN_CROSS_2026-10-13.md`. Zero
trials. Every data row re-runs from `scripts/cef/exec_capacity_open_cross.py`
(`fetch`, then `report`); its row-level cache is gitignored, because the data
is vendor data and the repository is public.

| claim / series | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| **What carries auction volume on the consolidated tape.** Sale condition `6` "Market Center Closing Trade" and `O` "Market Center Opening Trade" update volume; `M` "Market Center Official Close" and `Q` "Market Center Official Open" do **not** (volume column "NO"). So the closing cross is the primary's `6` print, and `M` is a price message | SIAC, *CTS Pillar Multicast Output Binary Specification* v2.11b, 2026-01-29, sale-condition table; `https://www.ctaplan.com/publicdocs/ctaplan/CTS_Pillar_Output_Specification.pdf`, fetched 2026-10-13 | [V] |
| Alpaca's code names agree: `6` Market Center Closing Trade, `M` Market Center Official Close, `O` Market Center Opening Trade, `Q` Market Center Official Open, `I` Odd Lot Trade | `GET data.alpaca.markets/v2/stocks/meta/conditions/trade?tape=A`, read 2026-10-13 | [V] |
| **On a day with no round-lot cross, `M` still carries a size, and it is not the cross.** PCN 2021-04-07: the cross was a 74-share odd lot (N print coded `I`, 16:00:02.0807); `M` said 270, the size of the 15:59:44 regular trade. MHD and PDO on 2021-04-07 / 04-05: no N trade after the bell at all; `M` = 100 at the last sale's price (a TRF print for MHD). On round-cross days `M` repeats the `6` size exactly (4,993 of 4,993 name-days, 2016–22). So `/v2/stocks/auctions` alone cannot separate an odd-lot cross from no cross | `GET /v2/stocks/trades` (feed=sip) around 16:00 on those dates, and the 2016–22 auction cache; read 2026-10-13 | [V] |
| **`/v2/stocks/auctions` lists one `6` per name-day** (0 of 19,960 N name-days in 2016–22 carry two). **The tape itself often repeats it**: the same size about 10–40ms later on every sampled closing name-day before 2020-11-04 and on 385 of 433 from then to 2022, never in the 2026 window. Read as one cross printed twice (identical sizes every time); whether the legacy tape counted the copy in volume is [U]. With that allowed for, the `6` size equals the primary's own cross trades on 1,409 of 1,409 sampled 2016–22 closes and 779 of 779 in 2026 | `GET /v2/stocks/trades` (feed=sip) around each auction, every 14th fit session and the last 60 sessions; `scripts/cef/exec_capacity_open_cross.py`, read 2026-10-13 | [V] counts; [U] legacy volume treatment |
| **Alpaca `time_in_force=opg`**: "eligible to execute only in the market opening auction"; "OPG orders submitted after 9:28am but before 7:00pm ET will be rejected"; after 7:00pm they are "queued and routed to the following day's opening auction"; "On open/on close orders are routed to the primary exchange"; they "do not necessarily execute exactly at 9:30am". **Fractional orders: DAY only** (OPG and CLS "No"). Extended-hours eligibility: limit DAY/GTC only. The page says nothing about short sales under OPG, and nothing about an Elite requirement | docs.alpaca.markets/docs/orders-at-alpaca ("Placing Orders"), `updatedAt` 2026-08-10, fetched 2026-10-13 | [V] |
| **Sources disagree on availability.** Alpaca Learn says "OPG and CLS orders are only available to Elite Smart Router users". The current docs (row above) do not. This paper account accepted `cls` on 2026-09-29 (11 of 13 then expired, `CLAUDE.md` rule 3). Whether it, or a live non-Elite account, accepts `opg` has **not** been tested and must not be tested without the team lead | alpaca.markets/learn/13-order-types-you-should-know-about (dated 2022-03-04), fetched 2026-10-13 | [V] that each page says it; **[U]** which binds |
| Paper fills "based on the real-time quotes", against "the best available current market price (NBBO)"; random partial fills 10% of the time; does not simulate market impact, price improvement, regulatory fees or dividends; the page does not mention auctions | docs.alpaca.markets/docs/paper-trading, `updatedAt` 2026-07-07, fetched 2026-10-13 | [V] |

| **NYSE opening auction (the primary for all 13 names)**: Pillar gateways open for order entry at 6:30 a.m.; "Orders can be entered and cancelled until the security is opened by the DMM, which may occur after 9:30 a.m."; imbalance publication from 8:00 a.m., every second. Closing: MOC/LOC entry cut-off 3:50 p.m. | `https://www.nyse.com/trade/auctions`, fetched 2026-10-13 (a timetable page, not rule text) | [V] page |
| **NYSE Rule 7.35A(f)** as approved: "there is no Auction Imbalance Freeze for a Core Open Auction or Trading Halt Auction and no restrictions on entry or cancellation of Auction-Only Orders before a Core Open Auction" | SEC Rel. 34-85962 (SR-NYSE-2019-05), 2019-05-29, `https://www.sec.gov/rules/sro/nyse/2019/34-85962.pdf`, fetched 2026-10-13 | [V] approval text; [S] that the current rulebook still reads so (the current text was not re-read; the 2026 timetable agrees) |

### 12s. The stale-NAV gate, replayed on the laptop's decides (added 2026-10-13)

The sources behind `docs/RUNNER.md` "As built (2026-10-13): the stale-NAV gate" and
its `docs/SYSTEM.md` §5 ruling. Report only; zero trials. Every figure re-runs from
`python3 scripts/data/nav_gate_replay.py --state-dir <laptop state>` (tables in
`results/cef/nav_gate_replay/`). Inputs: the laptop's prod state (read-only), the
2026-10-12 note's z-inversion table (§12n), CEFConnect and yfinance as served on
2026-10-13 (fetched 2026-10-14T02:59Z), and the local NAV panel (laptop copy, NAV to
2026-09-25, read-only).

| claim / series | source and fetcher | status |
|---|---|---|
| **Under the redesigned gate (`on`), every evening decide whose CEFConnect state the collector measured, five of them, defers to the morning backstop.** At the final check, best case (CEFConnect has D): nothing held. That is what the one decide made with CEFConnect present (2026-10-01, 12:00 ET) does: 5 replaced, 3 kept. Worst case (CEFConnect still has no row): every triggered name keeps its shares. On 2026-10-05 that is 9 names, leaving 4 eligible, so the whole book holds that day; on 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-02 the same is possible. No date flattens the book | `nav_gate_replay.py` (`replay`), on the state dir, the §12n table and CEFConnect's D−1 and D for all 13 names (one request each) | [V] on the laptop's decides; the assumption that the panel's D−1 row at decide time was yfinance's final NAV(D−1) is [U] |
| **The second trigger (NAV(D) = CEFConnect's NAV(D−1)) adds no name in the live period**: yfinance as served now equals CEFConnect there. **On the local panel as captured 2026-09-28 it catches PDI** (09-25 = 15.06 against a previous row of 15.12; CEFConnect 09-24 = 15.06, 09-25 = 15.10), with DSL (11.11 = 11.11, CEFConnect 11.10) and BIT (a genuine repeat) | `nav_gate_replay.py` (`panel_capture_check`): the gate's own `judge` on `data/cef/cef_nav.parquet`, read-only | [V] |
| **Double-checks**: CEFConnect's D as served 2026-10-13 equals the 2026-10-12 read on all 104 (date, name) pairs; yfinance as served 2026-10-13 equals the note's NAV(D) and NAV(D−1) on all 104 rows | same script | [V] |
| *Superseded design, kept as the record of why it changed:* **Replay of the 2026-10-13 stale-NAV gate** on the laptop's decides (as-of 2026-09-28 → 2026-10-07). Five evening decides had CEFConnect's state measured by the collector, and all five found no CEFConnect row for D. There, the drop rule drops every repeat: 2–9 identified repeats an evening (up to 11 counting names whose decide-time NAV the printed z cannot identify). On 2026-10-05 (decided 00:00 ET) that is 9 names, leaving 4 eligible, below `min_names`. The one decide made when CEFConnect had the row (2026-10-01, 12:00 ET on D+1) replaces 5 and drops none. CEFConnect as served 2026-10-13 equals the 2026-10-12 read on all 66 values compared; yfinance as served 2026-10-13 equals the note's NAV(D) and NAV(D−1) on all 104 rows | `scripts/data/nav_gate_replay.py` as of commit 200f1d1 (`git show 200f1d1:scripts/data/nav_gate_replay.py`), with the note's `live_period` table (z inversion), CEFConnect and yfinance fetched 2026-10-13T22:40Z | [V] on the laptop's decides; the assumption that the panel's D−1 row at decide time was yfinance's final NAV(D−1) is [U] |

### 12t. Alpha-leakage pass: the web claims (added 2026-10-13)

The external claims behind `results/cef/ALPHA_LEAKAGE_2026-10-13.md`. A deep-research
workflow fetched 24 sources, extracted 108 claims and put 25 of them to a three-vote
adversarial check (16 confirmed, 9 refuted). Votes are the verifiers'; each verifier
fetched the source. Report only; zero trials.

| claim | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| **Monthly equal-weight quintile long/short on CEF premium** (long the lowest-premium quintile, short the highest; about 25 funds per quintile), out of sample 1998-02 → 2011-12: **14.9%/yr gross, Sharpe 1.519** (t 5.67). Fixed-income RADF variant: five-factor alpha 17.2%/yr. The two halves do not differ (18.8% vs 17.5%, t 0.26). Ranked and formed at the same month-end close (no lag); turnover 2.3×/yr; long/short beta −0.079 (t −2.14). Whether a journal version exists (JFR 40(2), 2017) is disputed between verifiers; the 2014 working-paper figures were read directly | Patro, Piccotti & Wu, working paper 2014-10-02, Tables V, XI, XII (gc.cuny.edu PDF) | [V] 3–0 |
| **After Lehman, bond-CEF prices Granger-caused NAVs** (F 13.49, p 0.000), **not the reverse** (F 1.23, p 0.30); before the crisis NAV led price (F 19.47). 16 bond CEFs, 2004-03 → 2011-02; the authors call it an informal test | Anderson, Beard, Kim & Stern, JFSR 50(3), 2016 (Auburn WP 2014-14, Table VIII) | [V] 3–0 |
| **Adopting a managed distribution policy narrowed the discount by 5.29pp (mean) against matched controls**, persisting; 49 post-inception adoptions, 1995–2006; MDP funds sit about 6.7pp below peers in 2001–06 | Cherkes, Sagi & Wang, JFQA 49(5–6), 2014, supplementary Table A8 | [V] 3–0 |
| Funds with cut, variable or infrequent distributions trade at deeper discounts | Kazemi, SSRN 5429374 (2025, rev. 2026-04), abstract only, single author, unrefereed | [U] |
| **Cross-sectional differences in leverage are priced into closed-end bond-fund discounts** | Elton, Gruber, Blake & Shachar, JFQA 48(2), 2013, abstract | [V] abstract, 2–1; magnitude [U] |
| **The muni-CEF January effect is tax-loss selling at the prior year-end, in fund prices, not the assets**: January dummy 2.40% (t 2.74); 168 funds, 1990–2000. A 2024 replication finds it stronger | Starks, Yong & Zheng, JF 61(6), 2006; Carrion & Zhang, JFR 47(4), 2024 | [V] abstract, 2–1; figures and replication [S] |
| **Alpaca: $0 borrow and $0 locate fees on easy-to-borrow shorts for Trading API users from 2025-10-01.** Hard-to-borrow shorts need a single-use, non-refundable locate in round lots of 100, and pay rate ÷ 360 a day on market value, on the end-of-day quantity rounded up to 100, with weekends charged. Open short orders in a name that flips ETB → HTB overnight are cancelled before the open. HTB shorting launched 2026-06-24. Alpaca reserves the right to charge non-retail flow | Alpaca blog (zero borrow fees), support "short-selling fees", docs "margin and short selling", Brokerage Fee Schedule (revised 2026-10-12), blog "HTB trading API locates" | [V] 3–0 |
| **Alpaca High-Yield Cash: up to 3.56% APY, variable (as of 2026-09-22), on eligible uninvested USD cash**, for enrolled Trading API users who are not pattern day traders. The FDIC sweep supports Individual Taxable and Custodial accounts only. **Nothing published says short proceeds or a margin credit balance are eligible** | Alpaca blog (High-Yield Cash for Trading API), alpaca.markets/trading-api, docs "FDIC sweep program" | [V] 3–0; short-proceeds coverage a gap |
| **Alpaca `cls`: rejected 15:50–19:00 ET; after 19:00 queued for the next close; unfilled orders cancelled after the close. Limit-on-close is supported.** Alpaca Learn restricts OPG/CLS to "Elite Smart Router" users, and the order table marks MOC/LOC "Yes*, contact sales". A 2021 forum report says unfilled LOC was not cancelled | docs "orders at Alpaca" (updated 2026-08-10); Alpaca Learn "13 order types"; forum thread 5141 | [V] 3–0 cutoffs; LOC 2–1; access [U] |
| **Reg SHO Rule 201**: a 10% or greater decline from the prior close triggers a restriction for the rest of the day and the next; short sales may not execute at or below the national best bid. NYSE re-prices short orders in the closing auction to one tick above the bid | 83 FR 62646 (Rel. 34-84670); SEC Rule 201 FAQ Q1.2, Q2.1; SR-NYSEArca 34-85265 (2019) | [V] rule; NYSE re-pricing [S] (current Pillar text not fetched) |
| **FINRA portfolio margin stresses each single equity at ±15% (ten points), with no offsets between different single names**; cross-class offsets exist only for index product groups | FINRA Rule 4210(g)(2)(F), (g)(7), (g)(8); 17 CFR 240.15c3-1a(b)(1)(v)(B); Regulatory Notice 26-10 | [V] 3–0; house add-ons and broker minimums [U] |
| **Refuted, do not cite:** a 7.7-month discount half-life (Patro et al., 0–3); a bond-CEF price–NAV DCC break after 2008 (0–3); that a z-score misreads the muni January effect (0–3); MDP abstract claim (1–2); square-root closing-auction impact (18.2/7.4/4.5bp at 1% ADV, JFQA 2026, 0–3); auction cheaper than continuous trading (0–3); the NYSE imbalance-side study (0–3); "Alpaca opens shorts only in ETB names" (0–3); 26 USC 263(h) on in-lieu payments (0–3) | as listed | refuted |

### 12u. The portfolio framework: method citations (added 2026-10-15)

Cited by `results/cef/PORTFOLIO_FRAMEWORK_2026-10-15.md` §2.3–§2.4. Methods only;
no figure from these sources enters a decision.

| claim | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| **Shrink the sample covariance toward the constant-correlation model**: the estimator is the convex combination δF + (1 − δ)S, where F keeps the sample variances on its diagonal and sets every correlation to the average sample correlation; the optimal intensity δ is given in closed form. The paper motivates it with the sample matrix's "error-maximization" in a mean-variance optimiser (Michaud 1989, as cited there) | Ledoit & Wolf, "Honey, I Shrunk the Sample Covariance Matrix", working paper June 2003, Universitat Pompeu Fabra #691 (`econ-papers.upf.edu/papers/691.pdf`), §3.1–§3.2 read 2026-10-15; published *J. Portfolio Management* 30(4), 2004 | [V] method, from the working paper; [S] the journal citation (a search result) |
| *Negative, recorded so it is not cited from memory:* "errors in the means matter about ten times more than errors in the covariances" (attributed to Chopra & Ziemba 1993, *J. Portfolio Management* 19(2)). A search on 2026-10-15 found the paper but not the ratio, and a 2003 Lund working paper that revisits it reports different magnitudes | search only; the paper was not read | [U]: not cited by the framework |

docs.kalshi.com, and from the Wayback Machine. Whether the kalshi.com copies
differ is [U].

  those rows are [V] as of 2026-10-18 for exactly the facts quoted.

| claim / source | where | status |
|---|---|---|
| **Who is a SPAC.** Candidates: CIKs with SIC 6770 today (3,347) plus 424B4 filers from 2014 on (4,704): 7,125 in all. 1,489 pass the trust-account prospectus test, or have XBRL trust ≥ 90% of assets.
