Liquidation 8-Ks: Social Capital Hedosophia IV (0001104659-22-101307), VectoIQ II (0001104659-22-124142) | `spac_mechanics.py` | [V], 48 quotes |
| **Trust mechanics.** $10.00 per unit is deposited (RRE, Cerberus). The trust holds T-bills of 185 days or less or Rule 2a-7 funds (RRE), or bank deposits (JATT II). The payout is net of taxes and of up to $100,000 of interest for dissolution. Some SPACs allow working-capital draws, up to $500,000 a year at Inflection Point VI. Third-party claims can cut the trust below "the lesser of (i) $10.00 per public share and (ii) the actual amount per public share" (CHP) | the sample's filings | [V] |
| **Redemption rights.** A holder need not own on the record date, nor vote (RRE, JATT II, Maywood 2, CHP). One holder may redeem at most 15% of the shares sold (RRE) or 20% (JATT II). The shares are tendered by DWAC to the transfer agent up to two business days before the vote (RRE, Iron Horse). The transfer agent charges the broker about $80–$100, and it is "up to the broker whether or not to pass this cost on" (RRE, Maywood 2, Bain GSS, ChaSerg). At a liquidation, a street-name holder "will not need to take any action" (Social Capital IV) | same | [V] |
| **Extension votes are redemption windows at the trust**: CHP about $10.06, Aurora $10.2178, Zalatoris II about $11.15. Sponsors add sweeteners: Zalatoris II pays the lesser of $75,000 or $0.025 a share per monthly extension. **Some extensions need no vote**: Bain GSS runs 27 months with a letter of intent; Iron Horse extends by monthly sponsor deposits, up to 12 times | same | [V] |
| **Alpaca's fee for a voluntary corporate action: CONFIRMED at $100, and it applies to Alpaca Securities' own (Trading API) accounts.** Alpaca's brokerage fee schedule (revised 2026-10-12) lists "Voluntary Corporate Actions Processing $100 per unique election". The same schedule lists DWAC transfers at $150 per security and a DWAC reject at $100: position-transfer fees, and it does not say whether either applies to a redemption tender. A business partner's clients are referred to the partner's own schedule. **This settles the [U] on §12k's row "Voluntary corporate actions … cost $100 per client"** | `https://files.alpaca.markets/disclosures/library/BrokFeeSched.pdf` (sha256 814767d3…, fetched 2026-10-18) | [V] fee; [U] DWAC applicability |
| **Alpaca voluntary corporate actions** (Broker API FAQ). A third-party vendor e-mails each customer "with details and instructions". "The cost is $100 per client per voluntary corporate action", and "Alpaca can reject a customer's decision to act if there is no cash in the account". The listed types are tender / Dutch auction / purchase, conversion / exchange, election merger, consent, rights and warrant exercise. **SPAC redemption is not named.** Elected shares move to a contra security, and the cash is booked when DTC allocates, "normally a week or two after the client's election" | `https://docs.alpaca.markets/us/docs/voluntary-corporate-actions` (`.md`; updatedAt 2025-09-24; fetched 2026-10-18) | [V] page; [U] whether SPAC redemptions are offered |
| **Alpaca support FAQ.** Clients get a notification and "a link that allows them to make an election". ### 12ae. Merger arbitrage reconstructed for D4: event-time hazard, E1 at IBKR (added 2026-10-19)

The sources behind `results/events/MERGER_ARB_V2_2026-10-19.md` (team lead's ruling of
2026-10-19 on §12aa's note; zero trials). Every data row re-runs offline from
`python3 scripts/events/merger_arb_v2_feasibility.py --today 2026-10-19`, which reads committed
files and `data/` panels only (no network, no credentials) and reads no price or return of any
deal. Papers were fetched and read 2026-10-19.

**Data**

| claim / series | source and reader | status |

| Deal census 2010–2022 (announcement document dates, Form 25 completion dates, class, tender, acquirer CIK) and the pending deals' Alpaca flags (read 2026-10-18), IBKR fees (file `#BOF\|2026.10.16`) and 63-session ADV | `results/events/merger_arb/edgar_deals.csv`, `pending_deals_2026-10-18.csv`, built by `scripts/events/merger_arb_feasibility.py` (§12aa) | [V] as committed; flags and fees true on their dates only |
| IBKR FEE_RATE daily panel, 43 universe names, 2021-09-13 → 2026-09-09; muni CEF fees in the CEF/BDC merger windows | `data/cef/cef_borrow_history.parquet` (`scripts/cef/fetch_borrow_history.py`, §4/H4) | [V] levels; a cost, not a return |
| Universe muni acquirer merger windows (first and last merger filing) | `results/events/g1a_summary_2026-10-13.json` (`scripts/events/g1a_shortability.py`, §12p) | [S] |
| E1's muni effect (median acquirer-pair return, +167bp) and median hold (168 sessions) | `results/events/e1_etf_hedge_summary_2026-10-15.json` (`scripts/events/e1_etf_hedge_gate.py`, §12w; CRSP pre-2023, not re-run here) | [S] |
| Power arithmetic (P(alpha t ≥ 3), `_dsr` at N = 1) | `scripts/xasset/slate_feasibility.py` (`p_alpha_t`, `power`), reused unchanged | [V] arithmetic |

**Literature**

| claim | source | status |
|---|---|---|
| 3,414 cash and 1,963 equity one-step mergers, 1970–2010 (Mitchell–Pulvino data to 1995, Thomson One after), no tender offers or collars; 70% / 77% complete, median 83 / 97 days. Completion hazard hump-shaped (cash about 7%/week near week 20; equity 8% at week 25), withdrawal hazard flat. Window cutoffs cash weeks 11 and 36, equity 15 and 41. FF3 alphas per month, cash: Low 1 −0.0005 (0.0015), High 0.0064 (0.0016), Low 2 −0.0007 (0.0046), Buy&Hold 0.0038 (0.0013), High > Low p 0.0084; equity 0.0068, 0.0146, −0.0035, 0.0109, p 0.0002; High > Buy&Hold (joint) p 0.00615 (Table 3). Late sample 1991–2010 with early cutoffs: cash High 0.0043 (0.0024), Low 1 −0.0003, p 0.2077; equity p 0.0129; joint 0.0271 (Table 4 Panel A). Betas: cash High MKT 0.2975 (0.0668), Low 1 0.2088 (0.0507), High > Low 1 p 0.1186 (Table 5); betas unrelated to the hazard (Table 6); in months with MKT < −3% cash High 0.4635, Low 1 0.4518 (Table 7A); option factors small (Table 7B); High skewness 4.37. Cash small targets High 0.0113 (0.0024) against large 0.0020 (0.0021), Low 1 small 0.0001 (0.0021), Buy&Hold small 0.0069 against large 0.0000 (Table 8). Pooled, costs: High 0.0091 → 0.0035 (price impact) → −0.0011 (bid-ask), High > Low p 0.0015 / 0.0000 / 0.0025 (Table 9). Last-day purge: cash High 37bp, test p 0.004. Implied market hazard has "approximately 25 percent less variation"; the mispricing comes from "a subset of investors, possibly small retail investors"; tender offers excluded because the passage of time near expiry "is likely to be obvious to market participants"; projected completion dates of "limited informativeness" (footnote 5) | Giglio & Shue, "No News Is News: Do Markets Underreact to Nothing?", NBER WP 18914 (March 2013), `https://www.nber.org/system/files/working_papers/w18914/w18914.pdf`; published *RFS* 27(12):3389–3440 (2014) | [V] working paper read in full; [S] that the published tables match. The assignment of the late High > Buy&Hold p 0.12055 to Table 4 Panel A is read from a garbled text layout [S] |
| 2,130 mergers 1994–2000. Fixed-ratio stock acquirers: announcement [−1,+1] CAAR −2.73% (t −10.57, N 1,086), closing +1.18% (t 5.61), [A−20, C+20] +0.41% (t 0.38); cash acquirers +0.96% (t 3.48) and +5.58% (t 4.92). Floating-ratio pricing period −3.18% (t −2.79, N 59), then +2.53% (t 2.09). S&P acquirer of a non-S&P target in a stock deal: run-up 3.36% (t 5.20, N 166), reversal −1.32% (t −1.44); same-index stock mergers +0.26%, cash mergers +0.03% (Table IV). Median rise in acquirer short interest 40% (fixed-ratio deals) | Mitchell, Pulvino & Stafford (2004), *JF* 59(1):31–63, `https://www.johnhcochrane.com/s/mitchell_price_pressure_JF.pdf` | [V] Tables II and IV read (§12aa had the abstract only) |
| S&P 500 addition abnormal return 3.4% (1980s), 7.6% (1990s), 0.8% (2010–20); deletions −0.6% in 2010–20 | Greenwood & Sammon, "The Disappearing Index Effect", NBER WP 30748 (December 2022), `https://www.nber.org/system/files/working_papers/w30748/w30748.pdf`; published *JF* 80(2):657–698 (2025) | [V] working-paper abstract; [S] journal version |
| Returns 58% lower after publication (the 0.42 keep) | McLean & Pontiff (2016), §12b | [S] here |

**Tried and not used.** A Tsinghua-hosted PDF that a search returned for Giglio & Shue is a CV, not
the paper. An IJBE paper on target returns after cancelled acquisitions (1992–99) returned HTTP 403;

### 12aj. The event engine's free pilot: rules baseline and subagent pilot (added 2026-10-19)

The sources behind `results/events/EVENT_ENGINE_PILOT_2026-10-19.md`.

- **Zero trials.**
- **No API call, no key, no network.** Every input is the 50 EDGAR texts and
  submissions cached on 2026-10-15 for §12z. Each text was checked against
  `sample_manifest.csv`'s sha256 (50 of 50), and so was a re-render of each
  raw submission.

| claim / source | where | status |
|---|---|---|
| **The rules read the filings' own cover conventions.** These are the Schedule TO cover's "Check the following box if the filing is a final amendment reporting the results of the tender offer" (printed with `[x]`, `[X]` or `☒`, after or before the label), its "(Title of Class of Securities)" caption, and the 424B rights covers' "(the "Record Date")" and "Subscription Price" wording. They were read in the 25 dev filings only | the sample's EDGAR filings (accessions in `results/events/event_engine/sample_manifest.csv`; split in `scripts/events/event_engine_rules.py::split`) | [V] for those filings; other filers' typesetting [U] |
| **The gold was built by Claude Opus 5.5 readers and adjudicated by the same model.** Two blind passes were reconciled with written decisions, and 40 agreed cells were spot-checked by the same model (39 right) | `results/events/EVENT_ENGINE_DESIGN_2026-10-15.md` §7.2; `scripts/events/event_engine_reconcile.py` | [V] as recorded there |
| **Subagent models are chosen by alias.** The Agent tool's `model` parameter accepts `haiku`, `sonnet` and `opus`; which model version an alias serves on the subscription is not stated | the Agent tool schema exposed to this session, 2026-10-19 | [V] for the schema; the served version [U] (the collector records each batch's self-reported model ID) |
| **Tokens per character.** The pilot's batch sizes reuse §12z's estimate of 2.69 characters a token; nothing was counted | §12z; `scripts/events/event_engine_score.py` (`CHARS_PER_TOKEN`) | [S] |
